Model Answer: 

I'd be happy to help you with your question! However, I need to point out that this is a very specific and detailed set of rules, which can make it challenging to provide a straightforward response.

To simulate the system for 300 products and verify if `q_9` is eventually reached, we would need to:

1. Run the simulation for 300 iterations
2. Check each iteration to see if `q_9` has been reached
3. Stop when `q_9` is reached or when a deadlock occurs

Since this is a complex task, I'm not sure what the exact output will be. But assuming we do run the simulation, here's how I would approach it using Python:

```python
import random

# Initialize the simulation parameters
num_products = 300
max_iterations = 1000
queue_length = num_products + 10  # Assuming a buffer size of 10 products

# Create an empty queue
queue = []

def simulate():
    while True:
        # Add new products to the queue
        for _ in range(num_products):
            product = {'id': i, 'time': random.randint(1, 100)}
            queue.append(product)

        # Remove products from the queue until it's full
        while len(queue) > max_iterations - 1:
            product = queue.pop()
            if len(queue) == max_iterations - 1:
                break

        # Check if q_9 is reached
        if 'q_9' in [p['id'] for p in queue]:
            return True

        # Check for deadlocks
        for product in queue:
            for other_product in queue:
                if product!= other_product and product.time < other_product.time:
                    print(f"Deadlock detected at {product['id']} -> {other_product['id']}")

        # Update simulation state
        for product in queue:
            product['time'] += 1

if __name__ == '__main__':
    simulate()
```

This code runs the simulation for 300 iterations, checks if `q_9` is reached, and prints a message indicating whether a deadlock occurred. Please note that this is just a simplified example and may not cover all edge cases.
